Below we give an alternative, more formal definition of

\tau(\Gamma,\ell)
.

{defi}

Let
A
be a formula. We define two
unfoldings
of
A
, denoted by

{A^{\flat}}
and
{A^{\sharp}}
, to be flat sequents which are obtained as
follows. For each non-atomic subformula occurrence
B
of
A
, we
introduce a fresh propositional variable
z_{B}
. If
A
is atomic, then
{A^{\flat}}=A
and
{A^{\sharp}}=\varnothing
. Otherwise

{A^{\flat}}={[z_{A},{A^{\sharp}}]}
and
{A^{\sharp}}
is defined inductively as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\mathord{\mathbb{I}}^{\sharp}}&=&z^{\bot}_{\mathord{\mathbb{I}}}\\[4.30554pt]
{[{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}C}]^{\sharp}}&=&{[{z^{\bot}_{{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}C}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}[{z_{B}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}z_{C}}]},{B^{\sharp}},{C^{\sharp}}]}\\[4.30554pt]
{\langle{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}C}\rangle^{\sharp}}&=&{[{z^{\bot}_{{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}C}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}\langle{z_{B}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}z_{C}}\rangle},{B^{\sharp}},{C^{\sharp}}]}\\[4.30554pt]
{({B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C})^{\sharp}}&=&{[{z^{\bot}_{{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}({z_{B}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}z_{C}})},{B^{\sharp}},{C^{\sharp}}]}\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}

(3)

Let
\Gamma
be a sequent, and let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
be the formula
occurrences of
\Gamma
. Then we define

{\Gamma^{\flat}}={[\Gamma_{0},{A_{1}^{\sharp}},\ldots,{A_{n}^{\sharp}}]}
,
where
\Gamma_{0}
is
\Gamma
with every
A_{i}
replaced by
z_{A_{i}}
.

Remark 14
.

It is important to note that in Definition
2.4
every
subformula occurrence gets a fresh variable, in particular,
in
Equation

3
every occurrence of
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
in
A
is assigned
a fresh
z_{\mathord{\mathbb{I}}}
, and these variables have to be indexed
accordingly. Then the graph
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{RB}}(\Gamma)
is in fact

{\llbracket{\Gamma^{\flat}}\rrbracket}
equipped with a
B
-edge for every fresh

z
–
z^{\bot}
pair. When drawn as in Figure
3
, we label the vertical
B
-edges with the corresponding subformula
A
, and the lower vertex represents
z_{A}
and the upper vertex
z^{\bot}_{A}
.

{defi}

Let
\Gamma
be a sequent and
\ell
an axiom linking for

\Gamma
. We define the linking
{\ell^{\flat}}
for

{\Gamma^{\flat}}
to be the linking obtained from
\ell
by
mapping each fresh
z_{A}
to
z^{\bot}_{A}
, and vice versa. Then the

tree-like RB-prenet
of
\Gamma
and
\ell
is defined as

\tau(\Gamma,\ell)=\rho({\Gamma^{\flat}},{\ell^{\flat}})
.

The correctness criterion for tree-like RB-prenets is exactly the same
as for cographic RB-prenets, except that in a tree-like RB-prenet
every æ-cycle is automatically chordless. Therefore we have the following definition.

{defi}

A tree-like RB-prenet is
correct
if and only if it does not contain any
æ-cycle. A correct tree-like RB-prenet is also called a
tree-like
RB-net
.

{exa}

In Figure
3
, the left RB-prenet is correct, while the one on the right is not.

In
[
Ret99b
]
, Retoré has shown the equivalence of the two correctness criteria.

{thmC}

[
[
Ret99b
, Theorem 7]
]

For every sequent
\Gamma
and linking
\ell
, we have that

\rho(\Gamma,\ell)
is correct if and only if

\tau(\Gamma,\ell)
is correct.

In the remainder of this paper we will use the term
pomset logic
proof net
, or
proof net
for short, for correct prenets of both
kinds, i.e., for cographic RB-nets and tree-like RB-nets. This is
justified, as the two can be trivially transformed into each other in
linear time.

Pomset logic proof nets can easily be extended with cut. A
cut
in a sequent is a formula of the shape
C\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C^{\bot}
. Then the cut elimination theorem can be stated as follows:

{thmC}

[
[
Ret97a
, Theorem 7]
] Let
\Gamma
be a sequent formed by the formulas
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n},

C_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C_{1}^{\bot},\ldots,C_{k}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C_{k}^{\bot}
, and let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be obtained from
\Gamma
by removing
C_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C_{1}^{\bot},\ldots,C_{k}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C_{k}^{\bot}
.

If there is a pomset logic proof net for
\Gamma
, then there is also one for
\Gamma^{\prime}
.

2.5.
System
{\mathsf{BV}}
and the Calculus of Structures

System
{\mathsf{BV}}
, introduced by Guglielmi in
[
Gug99
,
Gug07
]
,
is a deductive system for the formulas defined in
Section

2.1
. It
is defined in the formalism called the
calculus of structures
,
and it works similar to a rewriting system, modulo the equational
theory defined in
Figure

1
.

The inference rules of
system
{\mathsf{BV}}
and its symmetric version

system
{\mathsf{SBV}}
are shown in Figure
4
. These rules
have to be read as rewriting rule schemes, meaning that (1) the
variable
a
can be substituted by any atom, and the variables

A,B,C,D
can be substituted by any formula, and that (2) the rules
can be applied inside any (positive) context.

More formally, a
context

S\{\cdot\}
is a formula which
contains exactly one occurrence of the hole
\{\cdot\}
in place of an
atom:

S\{\cdot\}\quad\mathrel{\raise 0.4pt\hbox{::}{=}}\quad\{\cdot\}\mid{S\{\cdot\}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}A}\mid{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}S\{\cdot\}}\mid{S\{\cdot\}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}A}\mid{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}S\{\cdot\}}\mid{S\{\cdot\}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}A}\mid{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}S\{\cdot\}}

Given a context
S\{\cdot\}
and a formula
A
, we write
S\{A\}
to
denote the formula that is obtained from
S\{\cdot\}
by replacing the
hole
\{\cdot\}
with
A
.

{exa}

Let the context
S\{\cdot\}={\langle{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}\{\cdot\}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}b}\rangle\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}({c\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a^{\bot}})}
and the formula
A={c^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}d}

be given. Then
S\{A\}={\langle{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}({c^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}d})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}b}\rangle\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}({c\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a^{\bot}})}
.

Figure 4
.
System
{\mathsf{BV}}
(the first two columns) and
{\mathsf{SBV}}
(all 3 columns).

If

A

\scriptstyle\mathsf{r}\;

B

is an inference rule and
S\{\cdot\}
a
context, then

S\{A\}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{r}\;

S\{B\}

is an instance of
the rule. A
(proof) system
is a set of inference rules. We
write

A

\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\mathsf{S}\;

\scriptstyle\;\delta

B

, or more
concisely
A\vdash_{\mathsf{S}}^{\delta}B
, if there is a derivation from

A
to
B
using only rules from the system
\mathsf{S}
, and that
derivation is named
\delta
. If in that situation
A=\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
, then we
write it as

\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\mathsf{S}\;

\scriptstyle\;\delta

B

or simply as

~\vdash_{\mathsf{S}}^{\delta}B
and call
\delta
a
proof
of
B
.
In this case we say that
B
is
provable
in
\mathsf{S}
.
We also omit the annotation
\delta
when we want to assert the mere existence
of a derivation or proof, without giving it a name.

\mathord{\mathbb{I}}

\mathsf{ai}{\downarrow}

\mathsf{q}\mathord{\downarrow}

0.77498pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.46497pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}b\mathchoice{\mathclose{\hbox to14.9722pt{\hfil$\displaystyle\rangle$\hfil}}}{\mathclose{\hbox to14.9722pt{\hfil$\textstyle\rangle$\hfil}}}{\mathclose{\hbox to13.84439pt{\hfil$\scriptstyle\rangle$\hfil}}}{\mathclose{\hbox to11.2778pt{\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\rangle$\hfil}}}
, involving the equations of associativity and commututativity of
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.77498pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.77498pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.46497pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}
, and unit for
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.77498pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.77498pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.46497pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}
.

Figure

5
shows an example for a proof in
{\mathsf{BV}}
.

We now recall some basic properties of
{\mathsf{BV}}
and
{\mathsf{SBV}}
. First,
observe that the rules
\mathsf{ai}{\downarrow}
(called
atomic interaction down

or
axiom
) and
\mathsf{ai}{\uparrow}
(called
atomic interaction up
or

cut
) are in atomic form. Their general forms

\mathord{\mathbb{I}}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{i}\mathord{\downarrow}\;

{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}A^{\bot}}

and

{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}A^{\bot}}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{i}\mathord{\uparrow}\;

\mathord{\mathbb{I}}

(4)

are derivable.

{defi}

An inference rule
\mathsf{r}
is
derivable
in a system
\mathsf{S}
iff
for every instance

A

\scriptstyle\mathsf{r}\;

B

there is a derivation

A\vdash_{\mathsf{S}}B
. An inference rule
\mathsf{r}
is
admissible

for a system
\mathsf{S}
iff for every proof

\vdash_{\mathsf{S}\cup\{\mathsf{r}\}}{A}
there is a proof

\vdash_{\mathsf{S}}B
.

Proposition 15
.

The rule
\mathsf{i}\mathord{\downarrow}
is derivable in
{\mathsf{BV}}
, and the rule
\mathsf{i}\mathord{\uparrow}
is derivable in
{\mathsf{SBV}}
.

The proof is standard and can be found in many papers (e.g.,

[
Gug07
,
GS01
,
ATS19
]
).
We are now going to state the cut elimination property for
{\mathsf{BV}}
.

{defi}

Two system
\mathsf{S}_{1}
and
\mathsf{S}_{2}
are
equivalent
if they
prove the same formulas.

{thmC}

[
[
GS01
,
Gug07
]
]
Systems
{\mathsf{BV}}
and
{\mathsf{SBV}}
are equivalent.

A proof of this result can be found in
[
Gug07
]
and in
[
Str03b
]
.

{thmC}

[
[
GS01
,
Gug07
]
]
The general cut rule
\mathsf{i}\mathord{\uparrow}
is admissible for
{\mathsf{BV}}
.

This is an immediate corollary of Proposition
15
and
Theorem
2.5
. Finally, the relation between
{\mathsf{BV}}
and
{\mathsf{SBV}}

can be strengthened to the following statement:

Corollary 16
.

For any two formulas
A
and
B
, we have

~\vdash_{{\mathsf{BV}}}{A^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}B}~
iff
~A\vdash_{{\mathsf{SBV}}}B~
.

2.6.
Unit-Free Versions of
{\mathsf{BV}}
and
{\mathsf{SBV}}

One of the main reasons to study cut-free systems is to have a
deductive system that is suitable for doing proof search. However, due
to the versatility of the unit
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
in formulas, proof search in
plain
{\mathsf{BV}}
as it is shown in Figure
4
is not practical. In
order to reduce the non-determinism in
{\mathsf{BV}}
, Kahramanoğulları
proposed in
[
Kah04
]
a unit-free version of
{\mathsf{BV}}
which is
better suited for proof search. As this system is also easier to
handle for some results we show in this paper, we introduce
\mathsf{BVu}

below. For didactic reasons, we also introduce its symmetric version

\mathsf{SBVu}
.

The formulas for
\mathsf{BVu}
are the same as defined
in
Section

2.1
, except that we do not allow any occurrence of
the unit
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
. This means that we have to restrict the equivalence
defined in
Figure

1
to the unit-free formulas. We define the
relation
\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}
to be the smallest congruence generated by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}({B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C})}&\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}&{({A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}B})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C}\\
{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}[{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}C}]}&\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}&{[{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}B}]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}C}\\
{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}\langle{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}C}\rangle}&\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}&{\langle{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}B}\rangle\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}C}\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{rcl}{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}B}&\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}&{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}A}\\
{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}B}&\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}&{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}A}\\
&&\end{array}

(5)

The inference rules for
\mathsf{BVu}
and
\mathsf{SBVu}
are then shown in
Figure
6
. Note that the rule
\mathsf{ai^{\circ}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
has no premise. It
is an axiom that is used exactly once in a
proof
which in
\mathsf{BVu}
or

\mathsf{SBVu}
is a derivation without premise (as the unit
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
is not
present and cannot take this role). Likewise, the rule
\mathsf{ai^{\circ}\mkern-2.0mu}{\uparrow}
has no
conclusion. It is used exactly once in a
refutation
, which is a
derivation with empty conclusion.

Figure 6
.
System
\mathsf{BVu}
(first 3 columns) and system
\mathsf{SBVu}
(all 5 columns).

We have the following immediate results.

Proposition 17
.

Let
A
and
B
be unit-free formulas. We have
~A\vdash_{\mathsf{SBVu}}B~
iff
~A\vdash_{{\mathsf{SBV}}}B~
.

Proof 2.4
.

If we have a derivation
A\vdash_{\mathsf{SBVu}}B
, then we immediately
have a derivation
A\vdash_{{\mathsf{SBV}}}B
, as every rule in
\mathsf{SBVu}

(except for
\mathsf{ai^{\circ}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
and
\mathsf{ai^{\circ}\mkern-2.0mu}{\uparrow}
) is derivable in

{\mathsf{SBV}}
. Conversely, assume we have a derivation

A\vdash_{{\mathsf{SBV}}}^{\delta}B
. Then, in
\delta
, the unit
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
can
occur. Let
\delta^{\prime}
be obtained from
\delta
by deleting the unit

\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
everywhere. Then every instance of the rule
\equiv
becomes
an instance of
\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}
; every instance of
\mathsf{q}\mathord{\downarrow}
becomes an
instance of
\mathsf{q_{2}}{\downarrow}
or
\mathsf{q_{3}^{L}}{\downarrow}
or
\mathsf{q_{3}^{R}}{\downarrow}
or
\mathsf{q_{4}}{\downarrow}
or trivial
(i.e., premise and conclusion of the rule instance become equal); and
similarly for
\mathsf{s}
and
\mathsf{q}\mathord{\uparrow}
. However, an instance of
\mathsf{ai}{\downarrow}

can become an instance of
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow}
or
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{L}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
or
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{R}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
(which
are in
\mathsf{SBVu}
), or
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow}
which is shown on the left
below. Similarly, an instance of
\mathsf{ai}{\uparrow}
can become an instance of

\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\uparrow}
or
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{L}\mkern-2.0mu}{\uparrow}
, or
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{R}\mkern-2.0mu}{\uparrow}
, or
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\uparrow}
which is shown on
the right below:

B

\scriptstyle\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.26665pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow}\;

{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}B}

{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}B}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.26665pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\uparrow}\;

B

(6)

These rules are not in
\mathsf{SBVu}
, but they can be derived with

\{\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow},\mathsf{s_{2}}\}
and
\{\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\uparrow},\mathsf{s_{2}}\}
, respectively.

{propC}

[
[
Kah04
]
]
The systems
\mathsf{BVu}
and
{\mathsf{BV}}
are equivalent.

Proof 2.5
.

First, if we have a proof
~\vdash_{\mathsf{BVu}}A~
then we can simply
replace the top instance of
\mathsf{ai^{\circ}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
by
\mathsf{ai}{\downarrow}
and have a proof of

{\mathsf{BV}}
. Conversely, a proof in
{\mathsf{BV}}
can be transformed into a proof
of
\mathsf{BVu}
by replacing the axiom
\mathsf{ai^{\circ}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
by a
\mathsf{ai}{\downarrow}
and then
following the same procedure as in the previous proof.

From these propositions we can immediately obtain the cut
elimination results for
\mathsf{BVu}
via the corresponding results for
{\mathsf{BV}}
:

Corollary 18
.

The systems
\mathsf{BVu}
and
\mathsf{SBVu}
are equivalent.

Corollary 19
.

For any two unit-free formulas we have that

~\vdash_{\mathsf{BVu}}{A^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}B}~
iff
~A\vdash_{\mathsf{SBVu}}B~
.

Corollary 20
.

The general cut rules

{({A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}A^{\bot}})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}B}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{i^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.26665pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\uparrow}\;

B

{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}A^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}B}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{i^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.26665pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\uparrow}\;

B

{({A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}A^{\bot}})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}B}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{i^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.26665pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{L}\mkern-2.0mu}{\uparrow}\;

B

{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}({A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}A^{\bot}})}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{i^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.44443pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.26665pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{R}\mkern-2.0mu}{\uparrow}\;

B

(7)

are admissible for
\mathsf{BVu}
.

Remark 21
.

Our version of
\mathsf{BVu}
is slightly different from the one by
Kahramanoğulları. In
[
Kah04
]

the rule
\mathsf{s_{2}}
is absent, and instead the rule
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow}
shown
in
Equation

6
is part of the system. We chose our variation because it is better suited for the results of this paper (e.g. Theorem
2.7
and the proofs in Section
3
).
But it is easy to see that the
two variants of
\mathsf{BVu}
are equivalent: first, as we have mentioned
above, the rule
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow}
is derivable in
\{\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow},\mathsf{s_{2}}\}
, and
second, the rule
\mathsf{s_{2}}
is admissible if
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow}
is present. This
can be seen by an easy induction on the size of the derivation. However,
note that the same trick does not work for the rule
\mathsf{q_{2}}{\downarrow}
. This
rule cannot be shown admissible, as the formula

{\langle{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}[{b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}c}]}\rangle\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}\langle{[{a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}b^{\bot}}]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}c^{\bot}}\rangle}
is not provable in
\mathsf{BVu}

without
\mathsf{q_{2}}{\downarrow}
.

.

Proof 2.6

and

\mathsf{q_{3}^{L}}{\downarrow}
and
\mathsf{q_{3}^{R}}{\downarrow}
are derivable in
\{\mathsf{\hat{q}_{3}^{L}}{\downarrow},\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}\}
and
\{\mathsf{\hat{q}_{3}^{R}}{\downarrow},\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}\}
, respectively.

2.7.
\mathsf{SBVu}
and Dicograph Inclusions

To wrap up these long preliminaries, we recall here some useful results that
also provide some motivation for the rules of
{\mathsf{SBV}}
and
\mathsf{SBVu}
.
The starting observation is that the non-interaction rules preserve the atoms of
a formula, so they can be seen as digraph rewriting rules preserving the set of
vertices. The following results, due to Béchet, de Groote and
Retoré
[
BdGR97
]
, elucidate the combinatorial meaning of this rewriting
system.

First, let us consider the case of unit-free formulas
without the tensor
connective
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}
.
Recall that according to Proposition
10
, such formulas
modulo
\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}
correspond to
series-parallel orders
on their atom
occurrences.

{thmC}

[
[
BdGR97
, Propositions 3.2 and 4.1]
(reformulated)]

Let
A
and
B
be two unit-free and tensor-free linear generalized formulas
over some set
X
. Then the inclusion of edges
R_{{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}\supseteq R_{{\llbracket B\rrbracket}}
holds if and only if
A\vdash B
in the fragment of

\mathsf{BVu}
where the interaction rules and the rules involving tensors have been
excluded:
\{\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu},\mathsf{q_{2}}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q_{3}^{L}}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q_{3}^{R}}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q_{4}}{\downarrow}\}
.

Equivalently, this is also the non-interaction non-tensor fragment of
\mathsf{SBVu}
,
since all the rules of
\mathsf{SBVu}
that are not in
\mathsf{BVu}
contain tensors.

Proof 2.7
.

Let us explain how to connect this reformulation to the original statement
in
[
BdGR97
]
. In the latter, the inclusion
R_{{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}\supseteq R_{{\llbracket B\rrbracket}}
is characterized by a rewriting system on
series-parallel orders whose rules are listed
in
[
BdGR97
, Definition 3.1]
. Observe that, among those rules:

•

(a), (b), (c) and (d) correspond to
\mathsf{q_{2}}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q_{3}^{R}}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q_{3}^{L}}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q_{4}}{\downarrow}

respectively;

•

(e) and (h) express the reflexivity and transitivity of the rewriting
relation, which corresponds to the fact that a derivation is a sequence of
zero, one or more inference rules;

•

the remaining rules, namely (f) and (g), express the contextual closure
of the rules, whose counterpart in our setting is deep inference, i.e. the
possibility of instantiating a rule in a context
S\{\cdot\}
.

There remains a subtlety: we must use the
\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}
rule to turn formulas into
equivalent ones on which other rules may be applied, whereas this is left
implicit in the setting of
[
BdGR97
]
(since
\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}
on formulas
corresponds to equality of dicographs). The fact that
\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}
suffices is due
to the fact that
“the algebraic representation of any
[series-parallel] order is unique modulo the associativity of
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}

and the commutativity of
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}
”
(a quote from
[
BdGR97
, §2]
where
we adapted the notations for the connectives).

Next, we turn to unit-free formulas that may contain tensors; their associated
graphs are all dicographs. One would then expect the non-interaction fragment of

\mathsf{SBVu}
to characterize dicograph inclusion. However, this is not quite so,
since one rule must be added.

{thmC}
[
[
BdGR97
, §5]
(rephrased)]

Let
A
and
B
be two unit-free linear generalized formulas over a set
X
.
Then the inclusion of edges
R_{{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}\supseteq R_{{\llbracket B\rrbracket}}
holds if and only if
A\vdash B
in the fragment of

\mathsf{SBVu}
without the interaction rules, plus the following additional
rule:

{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 10.24438pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 3.7779pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{${[{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}B}]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}[{C\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}D}]}$}}}\kern 3.7779pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ws}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to141.11703pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${({A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}C})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}({B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}D})}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\quad(\text{\emph{weak switch}})

Remark 24
.

In
[
BdGR97
, §5]
, it is suggested that the proof of
Theorem
2.7
can be carried over to give a proof
of Theorem
2.7
. This is not immediately
true. Consider for example
A={({a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}b})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}c}
and

B={a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}[{b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}c}]}
. Clearly
R_{{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}\supseteq R_{{\llbracket B\rrbracket}}
, so there must be a derivation from
A
to

B
. To construct this derivation, the proof in
[
BdGR97
]
proceeds by induction on

\left|V_{{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}\right|=\left|V_{{\llbracket B\rrbracket}}\right|

and makes a case analysis on the main connectives of
A
and
B
. In our
case it is
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}
for both. But the argument that works for
series-parallel orders does not work for dicographs in general. In
our example, we have to go through
{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}c}
. However with some
adjustments, the proof does go through. Since this paper is already
quite long, we refrain from giving the details, as they are quite
straightforward, once the aforementioned problem is observed.

Remark 25
.

We shall see in
Section

3.1
that the rules of
{\mathsf{BV}}
preserve pomset
logic correctness. As Retoré noticed
[
Ret99b
, §5]
, this is

not
the case for the weak switch rule, and this provides one
justification for excluding it from
{\mathsf{SBV}}
.

Another argument, which is more intrinsic to
{\mathsf{BV}}
, is that
{\mathsf{BV}}+\mathsf{ws}
does
not admit cut-elimination, or equivalently, that
{\mathsf{SBV}}+\mathsf{ws}
is not
conservative over
{\mathsf{BV}}+\mathsf{ws}
. The exclusion of the weak switch for this reason
is explained as a deliberate design choice by Guglielmi in the discussion
concerning
“conservation laws”
at the beginning of
[
Gug07
, §3]
.

Remark 26
.

By analogy with the previous subsection, we could show that the
non-interaction fragment of
\mathsf{SBVu}+\mathsf{ws}
is equivalent over
unit-free formulas to
\{\equiv,\mathsf{q}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q}{\uparrow},\mathsf{ws}\}
with units (indeed,
the usual switch is an instance of the weak switch when one of the
formulas is set to a unit). Then,
Theorem
2.7
above becomes equivalent to

[
Gug07
, Conjecture 3.3.3]
, which states that

\{\equiv,\mathsf{q}{\downarrow},\mathsf{q}{\uparrow},\mathsf{ws}\}
characterizes dicograph inclusion. In
other words, that conjecture had been proved before it was stated.

3.
Comparing
{\mathsf{BV}}
and Pomset Logic

In this section we investigate the relation between the two logics,
{\mathsf{BV}}
and pomset logic. We
have already seen in Section
2.1
that every formula
uniquely determines a dicograph. Furthermore, by inspecting the rules of

{\mathsf{BV}}
in Figure
4
, one can see that the rule
\equiv
does
not change that dicograph, and that the rules
\mathsf{s}
and
\mathsf{q}\mathord{\downarrow}

only change the set of edges but not the set of vertices of the
corresponding dicograph. Additionally, every instance of
\mathsf{ai}{\downarrow}

removes one pair of dual atoms, and in a proof of
{\mathsf{BV}}
, every atom
occurring in the conclusion has to be removed by exactly one instance
of
\mathsf{ai}{\downarrow}
in the proof.

This means that every
{\mathsf{BV}}
proof
\delta
uniquely determines an axiom
linking
\ell(\delta)
for its conclusion, and hence by definition
also a pomset logic pre-proof, which in turn, by
Definition
2.4
, determines a cographic RB-prenet.

In Section
3.1
we are going to show that every
cographic RB-prenet that is obtained from a
{\mathsf{BV}}
proof in such a way
is indeed correct, and therefore every theorem of
{\mathsf{BV}}
is also a
theorem of pomset logic.

Then, in Section
3.2
we show that the converse
does not hold, i.e., there are theorems of pomset logic
that are not theorems of
{\mathsf{BV}}
.
We do this by presenting a formula that is provable in pomset logic
but not in
{\mathsf{BV}}
.

3.1.
{\mathsf{BV}}
is Contained in Pomset Logic

In this section we do not only show that every theorem of
{\mathsf{BV}}
is
also a theorem of pomset logic, but also that every proof in
{\mathsf{BV}}

uniquely determines a pomset logic proof net with the same conclusion.

The proof that we present here uses the basic idea
from
[
Str03b
]
that has also been used
in
[
Str03a
]
. In
[
Ret99b
]
, Retoré presents an
alternative method.

To begin, let
\delta
be a
{\mathsf{BV}}
proof of a formula
A
. We denote by

(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket\delta\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)=\rho(A,\ell(\delta))
the cographic RB-prenet
generated from
\delta
as described above (see
Definition
2.4
). Then the main result of this
section is the following.

Theorem 27
.

For every
{\mathsf{BV}}
proof
\delta
, the cographic RB-prenet

(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket\delta\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
is correct.

The basic idea of the proof for this theorm is to proceed by way of contradiction, showing that for every possible application of an inference rule of
{\mathsf{BV}}
, whenever the RB-prenet of the conclusion is incorrect, then already the premise of that rule instance must be incorrect. Since the premise of every proof is
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
(which is correct) we obtain our desired result. In order to make this argument formally precise and in order to keep the involved case analysis managable we need to introduce some more concepts.

First observe that every RB-dicograph uniquely determines a balanced formula,
up to renaming of variables and equivalence under
\equiv
. This gives
us immediately the following proposition.

Proposition 28
.

Let
\delta
be a proof in
{\mathsf{BV}}
. Then there is a balanced formula

A
, that is provable in
{\mathsf{BV}}
and such that
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket A\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
and
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket\delta\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
are isomorphic.

Proof 3.1
.

Let
B
be the conclusion of
\delta
. Then
A
is obtained from
B

by renaming all variable occurrences such that the result is
balanced and the linking is preserved.

{defi}

Let
A
and
B
be generalized formulas over a set
X
. We call
B

a
pseudo-subformula
of
A
, written as
B\sqsubseteq A
, if it is
equivalent under
\equiv
to some
A^{\prime}
that can be obtained from
A
by
replacing some occurrences of elements of
X
(in the case of usual formulas,
some atom occurrences) in
A
by
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
. If
B\sqsubseteq A
and

B\not\equiv A
, then we say that
B
is a
proper pseudo-subformula
of

A
, and write it as
B\sqsubset A
.

{exa}

We have the pseudo-subformula relation

{\langle{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}d}\rangle\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}({b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}b})}\sqsubset{\langle{({a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}d\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}f})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise coming from the equivalence

{\langle{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}b})}\equiv{\langle{({a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{I}}})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}d\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{I}}}\rangle\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}({b\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}[{({\mathord{\mathbb{I}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{I}}})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}\langle{\mathord{\mathbb{I}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}b}\rangle}]})}

The following proposition explains our choice to denote both pseudo-subformulas
and induced subgraphs (Definition
2.2
) by
\sqsubseteq
.

Proposition 29
.

Let
A
and
B
be
linear generalized
formulas (this is the case, for
instance, when
A
and
B
are
balanced
usual formulas). We have that

B\sqsubseteq A
if and only if
{\llbracket B\rrbracket}\sqsubseteq{\llbracket A\rrbracket}
, and
B\sqsubset A
if and only if
{\llbracket B\rrbracket}\sqsubset{\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.

Proof 3.2
.

Let
X=V_{\mathcal{{\llbracket A\rrbracket}}}
. By definition, the induced subgraphs
\mathcal{H}\sqsubseteq{\llbracket A\rrbracket}
are in bijection with the subsets
Y\subseteq X
via

V_{\mathcal{H}}=Y
. Let
A_{\restriction{}Y}
be the formula obtained from
A
by
replacing every
x\in X\setminus Y
by
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
; from the inductive
definition of
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}
one can check that

{\llbracket A_{\restriction{}Y}\rrbracket}=\mathcal{H}
. To conclude, apply Theorem
2.2
to
handle the equivalence
\equiv
that appears in the definition of
pseudo-subformula.

Lemma 30
.

Let
A
be a
balanced
formula (Definition
2.4
) and
B
be a

balanced
pseudo-subformula of
A
. If
A
is provable in
{\mathsf{BV}}
, then so
is
B
.

Proof 3.3
.

Let
\delta
be the proof of
A
in
{\mathsf{BV}}
, and let
\delta^{\prime}
be
obtained by replacing all atoms that do not occur in
B
in every
line of
\delta
by
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
. Then
\delta^{\prime}
is a valid derivation of
B

in
{\mathsf{BV}}
.

{defi}

Let
H
be a balanced formula and
\mathcal{H}=(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket H\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
. We say that
H
is a

(balanced) cycle
when the RB-digraph
\mathcal{H}
admits a
chordless

æ-cycle that visits all vertices
13
13

13

An elementary cycle in a digraph
that visits all vertices is generally called a
Hamiltonian cycle
.

and either
|V_{\mathcal{H}}|=2
or
R_{\mathcal{H}}\cap B_{\mathcal{H}}=\varnothing
. The
size

\left|H\right|
of a balanced cycle
H
is its size as a formula (Definition
2.1
). In particular, we have
\left|H\right|=|V_{\mathcal{H}}|
.

Proposition 31
.

A formula
H
is a balanced cycle if and only if there are pairwise distinct
atoms
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}
for some
n\geq 1
, such that

H\equiv{L_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}L_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}\cdots\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}L_{n}}
, where
L_{1}={a_{n}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a_{1}}
or

L_{1}={a_{n}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}a_{1}}
, and for every
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
we
have
L_{i}={a_{i-1}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a_{i}}
or

L_{i}={a_{i-1}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}a_{i}}
.

Proof 3.4
.

This follows almost immediately from the definitions.

{defi}

We say that a balanced formula
A

contains a cycle
if it has
a pseudo-subformula
B\sqsubseteq A
that is a cycle (or
equivalently, if
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket A\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
contains a chordless æ-cycle).

We are now ready to state and prove the main lemma, which is at the heart of the proof of Theorem
27
.

Lemma 32
.

Let

Q

\scriptstyle\mathsf{r}\;

P

be an instance of an inference rule in

\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
. If
P
is a balanced cycle then
Q
contains a cycle. If

\mathsf{r}\neq{\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}}
then the size of the cycle in
Q
is strictly
smaller than
\left|P\right|
.

Proof 3.5
.

By Proposition
31
we have that

P\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}{L_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}L_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}\ldots\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}L_{n}}
, where
L_{1}={a_{n}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a_{1}}
or

L_{1}={a_{n}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}a_{1}}
, and for every
i\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
we
have
L_{i}={a_{i-1}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a_{i}}
or

L_{i}={a_{i-1}^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}a_{i}}
, with all
a_{i}
being pairwise
distinct. We proceed by case analysis on the rule
\mathsf{r}
. First
observe that by Proposition
31
the rules
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}}\mkern-3.0mu}{\downarrow}
,

\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{L}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
,
\mathsf{ai^{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.62221pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.3733pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}_{R}\mkern-2.0mu}{\downarrow}
cannot be applied to
P
(seen bottom up), and
if
\mathsf{r}={\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}}
, then
Q
trivially contains a cycle, whose size
is equal to
\left|P\right|
. Now assume
\mathsf{r}

In all cases the size of the cycle in
Q
is strictly smaller than
\left|Q\right|=\left|P\right|
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
P
be a balanced formula that contains a cycle. Then
P
is not provable in
{\mathsf{BV}}
.

Proof 3.6
.

Let
H
be the cycle in
P
, and let
n=\left|H\right|
be its size. We
proceed by induction on
n
. Note that
n
has to be even. For

n=2
, we have that
H\equiv{a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}a}
or
H\equiv{a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}a}

for some atom
a
. By way of contradiction, assume
P
is provable
in
{\mathsf{BV}}
. By Lemma
30
,
H
is also provable in

{\mathsf{BV}}
, which is impossible. For the inductive case let now
n>2
. As
before, we have by Lemma
30
that
H
is provable in

{\mathsf{BV}}
. By Proposition
2.6
and
Proposition
23
,
H
is provable in
\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
. Let
\delta

be that proof in
\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
. Let now
Q
be the premise of the
bottom-most rule instance
\mathsf{r}
of
\delta
that is not a
\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}

(i.e., the conclusion of
\mathsf{r}
is
H^{\prime}\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}H
and
Q\not\mathrel{\equiv^{\prime}\mkern-2.0mu}H
). By Lemma
32
,
Q
contains a cycle whose size is
smaller than
n
. By induction hypothesis
Q
is not provable in

{\mathsf{BV}}
, and therefore also not provable in
\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
, which is a
contradiction to the existence to
\delta
.

We can now complete the proof of Theorem
27
.

Proof 3.7

(Proof of Theorem
27
)
.

Let
\delta
be a proof in
{\mathsf{BV}}
. By Proposition
28
,
there is a balanced formula
P
, such that

(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket P\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)=(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket\delta\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
, and such that
P
is provable in

{\mathsf{BV}}
. Now assume, by way of contradiction, that
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket\delta\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)

is incorrect. That means that
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket\delta\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
contains a
chordless æ-cycle, or equivalently, that
P
contains a cycle. By
Lemma
33
,
P
is not provable in

{\mathsf{BV}}
. Contradiction.

3.2.
Pomset Logic is not Contained in
{\mathsf{BV}}

In this section we present a formula that is provable in pomset logic, i.e., has a correct pomset logic proof net, but that is not provable in
{\mathsf{BV}}
. From what has been said in the previous section, it follows that if such a formula exists then there is also a balanced such formula. The formula we discuss in this section is the formula
Q

Figure 9.
Two further simplifications of the graph on the right of

Figure

8

To see that these are provable in pomset logic, we have to show that
the RB-prenets do not contain chordless æ-cycles. For this we focus
on the tree-like RB-prenet, because in tree-like RB-prenets all
æ-paths (and therefore also all æ-cycles) are chordless. Hence, it
suffices to show that there are no æ-cycles.

Observe that the

B
-edges corresponding to the roots of the formulas in
\Gamma_{Q}

cannot participate in an æ-cycle because they have no adjacent

R
-edge at the bottom. We can therefore remove each of these

B
-edges, together with the two adjacent
R
-edges at the top. The
resulting graph is shown on the right of
Figure
8
.

Another simplification we can do without affecting the æ-cycles
is replacing the two
B
-edges labeled
{a\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}b}
and

{c\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}d}
, together with the connecting
R
-edge by a single

B
-edge, and similarly for the two
B
-edges
{g\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}h}
and

{e\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}f}
. The result is shown on the left of
Figure
9
.

Finally, observe that there is no æ-cycle that passes trough the two

B
-edges labeled
b
and
a
. The reason is that the directed

R
-edge between them has the opposite direction of the two adjacent

R
-edges on the other endpoints of these
B
-edges. Thus, we can
collapse these two edges (and the adjacent
“triangle”
) to a single
vertex. The same can be done for the pairs
c/d
and
g/h
and

e/f
. We end up with the RB-graph on the right of
Figure
9
.

Proposition 34
.

The formula
Q
and the sequent
\Gamma_{Q}
shown in

Equation

10

and
Equation

11
above are provable in pomset
logic.

Proof 3.8
.

In the paragraphs above, we have argued that the tree-like RB-prenet
in Figure
7
has an æ-cycle if and
only if the RB-digraph on the right of
Figure
9
has an æ-cycle. Now it is easy to
see that this graph has no æ-cycle. Hence, tree-like RB-prenet for

\Gamma_{Q}
is correct.

Let us now show that the formula
Q
is not provable in
{\mathsf{BV}}
. To do
so we will show that whenever a
{\mathsf{BV}}
inference has as conclusion
Q

then its premise defines an incorrect RB-prenet in pomset logic, and
is therefore not provable in pomset logic. Since by
Theorem
27

all
{\mathsf{BV}}
proofs induce correct
pomset proof nets, we can conclude that those premises are not
{\mathsf{BV}}
-provable, therefore
there is no way to build a
{\mathsf{BV}}
-proof of
Q
.

The main difficulty here is to make sure that we do not overlook any
case when checking all possible inferences that have
Q
as
conclusion. Since the unit
\mathord{\mathbb{I}}
can make these kind of arguments
difficult to check, we use here
\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
.
Now observe that
Q
has no subformula of the form
x\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}x^{\bot}
. This means we only have to consider the non-axiom rules of
\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
.

To cut down the number of cases to consider, we take advantage of the symmetries of
Q
.
Let us first look at the
automorphisms
, i.e., permutations of the variables that
results in a formula
Q^{\prime}
with
Q^{\prime}\equiv Q
, which means
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)=(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket Q\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
. The following are automorphisms:

(
\alpha
)

a\leftrightarrow c
,
b\leftrightarrow d
,
e\leftrightarrow g
,
f\leftrightarrow h

(
\beta
)

a\mapsto e
,
b\mapsto f
,
c\mapsto g
,
d\mapsto h
,
e\mapsto c
,
f\mapsto d
,
g\mapsto a
,
h\mapsto b

The action of these automorphisms on the subformulas of
Q
of the form

{x^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}y^{\bot}}
is transitive:
\alpha({a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}h^{\bot}})={c^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}f^{\bot}}
,
\beta({a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}h^{\bot}})={e^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}b^{\bot}}
and

\alpha\circ\beta({a^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}h^{\bot}})={g^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}d^{\bot}}
.

Another useful symmetry is not quite an automorphism: it is the
following
anti-automorphism
:

(
\gamma
)

a\leftrightarrow h,\;b\leftrightarrow g,\;c\leftrightarrow f,\;d\leftrightarrow e

that sends
Q
to its
“conjugate”

{Q}^{\dagger}
defined inductively as follows:

{x}^{\dagger}=x\ \text{when $x$ is an atom}\qquad{(B\odot C)}^{\dagger}={C}^{\dagger}\odot{B}^{\dagger}\ \text{for}\ \odot\in\{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}\}

Note that the reversal of the arguments only matters for the non-commutative
connective
\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}
, and
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket{Q}^{\dagger}\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
is the same as
(\mkern-4.0mu\llbracket Q\rrbracket\mkern-4.0mu)
, except that all directed
R
-edges have the opposite direction. Thus, conjugacy preserves provability
both in pomset logic (reversing the direction of all cycles in the correctness
criterion) and in system
\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
(the inference rules are closed under conjugacy,
with
\mathsf{\hat{q}_{3}^{L}}{\downarrow}
and
\mathsf{\hat{q}_{3}^{R}}{\downarrow}
being swapped).

We will now go through all the rules of
\mathsf{BV\hat{u}}
and check all possible
applications. Using a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
32
, we will see that in each case there is a cycle in the resulting
premise.

•

{[{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}C}]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}[{B\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}D}]}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{q_{4}}{\downarrow}\;

{\langle{A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}B}\rangle\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}\langle{C\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}D}\rangle}

: Because of
the action of the automorphisms
\alpha/\beta
, we can without loss
of generality assume that
A=a^{\bot}
and
B=h^{\bot}
. There are three subcases:

–

C=e^{\bot}
and
D=b^{\bot}
. We get the cycle

{({e\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varotimes$}}}h})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bindnasrepma$}}}\langle{e^{\bot}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}h^{\bot}}\rangle}
in the premise of the

\mathsf{q_{4}}{\downarrow}
-application.
